| 1163 | 2026-04-10T14:00:30Z | Performance: AnimatedModesViz inner waveform loop computed `Math.cos(gamma*log_x[i] - omega*t)` for every (prime, x-step) pair on every animation frame — at the max slider setting (50 primes × 200 x-steps) that is 10,000 cosine evaluations per frame, or ~600k/s at 60 fps, the dominant cost of the Modes page. But `gamma*log_x[i]` only depends on zeroIndex (which changes on user interaction, not per-frame) and the envelope factor is also constant per i. Applied the angle-subtraction identity cos(a-b) = cos(a)cos(b) + sin(a)sin(b) and precomputed Float64Array tables of `cos(gamma*log_x[i])*envelope(i)` and `sin(gamma*log_x[i])*envelope(i)` in a useMemo keyed on zeroIndex. | 1166 | 2026-04-10T14:17:09Z | Bug: NyquistBodeViz.jsx stored the negative-frequency mirror of the Nyquist contour in decreasing-ω order (t=−1 → t=−60) instead of increasing-ω order (t=−60 → t=−1) because `points.map(p => ({t:-p.t, re:p.re, im:-p.im}))` preserved the source ordering while negating t. The curve shape is identical either way, but drawArrows() uses `pts[idx+1] − pts[idx−1]` for direction and draws four arrow heads along the path — so on the orange negative branch the arrows pointed AWAY from the origin (toward very negative t), while the positive branch arrows also pointed away from the origin (toward very positive t). For a Nyquist sweep ω ∈ [−∞, +∞] the canonical traversal has ω monotonically increasing through zero, so the negative branch arrows must point TOWARD the origin to match the positive branch; the mismatched orientation flipped the visual 'encirclement sense' of −1, which by the argument principle literally counts zeros off the critical line (the whole reason the plot is drawn). Atomic fix: `const negPoints = points.slice().reverse().map(p => ({ t: -p.t, re: p.re, im: -p.im }));` — the slice() prevents mutating `points`, the reverse() inverts the ordering so negPoints now runs t=−60 → t=−1, and the arrow directions on the negative branch now correctly point toward the origin, matching the positive branch and restoring the correct encirclement sense. Line shape, bounds, positive-branch arrows, start/end markers, unit circle, critical-point marker, axes, Bode plot, and nyquistData consumers are unchanged — only arrow orientation on the orange branch is corrected. Added a 6-line comment above the line explaining the Schwarz reflection and why reverse is required for correct ω ordering. Build verified | NyquistBodeViz.jsx |

| 1885 | 2026-04-24T09:30:00Z | imp-917 (proof, medium, honest-partial-ppp): add --batch-plan-cost-triple-iqr shell emitter to scripts/imp-917-run-cell.mjs, a second outlier-robust dispersion view complementary to --batch-plan-cost-triple-mad (partial-nnn). Where MAD reports the typical deviation magnitude from the median, IQR (Q3 - Q1) reports the span of the middle 50% of the distribution directly on the wall-time axis. The two views answer different operator questions (MAD = how far does a typical triple sit from the center; IQR = what range contains the middle half of the triples), with IQR mapping more directly to provisioning a shared-queue worker whose expected completion window is bounded by [Q1, Q3]. Both statistics are robust against the two N=10000 tail triples that inflate the population stddev (--batch-plan-cost-triple-stddev, partial-lll). Uses Tukey exclusive-midpoint halves: for even knownCount the two halves each contain knownCount/2 values; for odd knownCount the median value is excluded from both halves. On BATCH_PLAN's 6-row known set [1400, 2800, 18000, 18000, 72000, 72000] the lower half [1400, 2800, 18000] yields Q1=2800, the upper half [18000, 72000, 72000] yields Q3=72000, IQR=69200 s (~19.22 CPU-hours), median=18000 s, IQR/median=3.84. That 3.84 ratio sits substantially above the MAD/median ratio of 0.88 (partial-nnn) because IQR spans Q1-to-Q3 while MAD measures median-symmetric deviation-magnitude; both robust statistics agree the middle 50% is not tightly clustered, but IQR makes the absolute Q1-Q3 window explicit for the operator. Reuses computeBatchPlanCost() (partial-vv helper) with zero new math; unknown-N rows are pre-filtered before the split so nulls cannot corrupt either quartile, and a trailing "(K triple-run(s) with unknown N excluded from IQR)" note is appended when any are excluded. Handler-local medianOf(arr) helper applied three times (full sorted array for medianSec, lower half for Q1, upper half for Q3); even/odd midpoint selection at each call matches partial-jjj / partial-nnn. Two degenerate branches: all-unknown (knownCount===0) prints "all triples have unknown N" and exits 0 without touching the sort; knownCount===1 prints "only 1 known triple, need at least 2 to form quartiles" and also exits 0, matching the partial-zz / partial-ddd / partial-hhh / partial-jjj / partial-lll / partial-nnn degenerate cadence. Pure data emitter, exit=0 unconditionally (no validation gate), not wired into --preflight / --preflight-json.
